. *The own entry* is read with `GET /api/v1/directory/me` (new member `entry`) and written with `PUT` (memberships, one to
  five) or `POST /api/v1/directory/me/confirmation`; a saved change is a confirmation, the date is written either way.
  The page does not change the pseudonym.
. *`BINDING_CANDIDATES` and `NOT_BINDABLE` are `409`* with their members; the form keeps its candidates after
  `NOT_BINDABLE`. The statuses are the register's choice; the requirement names the outcomes only.
. *The event `catalog-changed`* refreshes an open list or entry sheet, as `document-saved` does.
. *Reading while the Git server is unreachable* serves the catalogs and the persons from the last fetched state; every
  write answers `GIT_UNAVAILABLE`.
. *The sheet of a service* shows the stored protection needs and the effective ones side by side, names the dependency
  that decides each dimension and lists the dependencies as the chain `service › depends on`; a cycle is a notice above
  the form. *The wizard* of the import is the template `WizardPage`; the entry in the lens is the action *Import*
  beside *New*.
. *The mock answers the writes with fixed ids* (the created entry, the created persons), because it does not read the
  bodies; the e2e flows type the same values.
. *The fixtures of the mock stay static* and are guarded by tests that fail on drift between a fixture and the source it
  mirrors (web-mock `README.adoc`, _Fixtures and Drift Tests_); the alternative is a scenario-format change that derives
  the lists when a scenario is loaded.
. *The first-login mock has two registered states:* `registered` (a new entry, pseudonym `p-9b7e21`) and
  `registered-bound` (the bound candidate, `p-3d7a15`); each answers `GET /api/v1/directory/me` with its entry.
. *The mock reads an import of up to 2 MiB* of descriptors (its transport cap is six times that, for JSON escaping); a
  body above the cap answers `413` with the `limit` of its request, 2097152 for the import and 65536 otherwise.
. *The Catalogs lens in the web app* decides these details, none of which the register fixed before: the first visit of a
  page session to `/catalogs` opens the first catalog in place of the address, a later visit shows the five catalogs;
  `POST` and `PUT` carry the entry as `{"value": {…}}`, the `PUT` with its `id` equal to the one of the address; the `id` of
  an entry that exists is read and never edited; an entry that someone else saved after the page read it replaces what the
  person has not touched, and a person with changes of their own is warned that saving replaces what was saved (the last
  save wins, point 4); a retired entry offers neither *Save* nor *Retire*; the form texts of the field errors `REQUIRED` and
  `ID_MISMATCH` are `form.error.required` and `form.error.id-mismatch`; the outcomes `ID_TAKEN` and `RETIRED` are the
  notices `error.id-taken` and `error.retired`.
. *The way up on the phone.* The top bar shows only the current step of the path below 768 px, the sheets carry their own
  way up and the bottom bar navigates; a visible parent link in the top bar is a shell-wide design question, recorded
  for the owner.
. *The pages of the directory in the web app* (`web-app/src/lib/views/directory/`) decide these details, none of which
  the register fixed before:
  ** *The persons page* reads `GET /api/v1/directory/persons` for every user with a right and asks again with `q` once the
  typing has paused for 300 ms; it narrows the received list by the typed text at once, because the mock ignores `q`
  and a complete answer may be narrowed by the client. The search is state of the page; the address admits no query.
  ** *The entry of persons* is one form of rows, each a name and one to five memberships (team and role, retired entries
  offered only where a row holds them). A row nothing was typed into is not sent, a name that is in the table already is
  a note and no refusal, and a refusal at `/persons/<n>/…` is placed at the row the person `<n>` was sent from. The erasure
  is offered for a person with a name only, asks first, and reads the table again after `404 NOT_FOUND`. Both
  `409 MANAGED_BY_SOURCE` answers (prefill and erasure) are one notice, and nothing else on the page changes.
  ** *The import wizard* reads the chosen files in the browser (`File.text()`), keeps one file of a name once, and names a
  pasted text `Pasted text <n>`. The limits (100 documents, 2 MiB of UTF-8 together) are checked before the request and
  the `limit` of a `413` is shown. A `422` pointer `/documents/<n>/…` marks document `<n>`. An import that would create and
  update nothing is not offered. The entries created or updated are linked only after the import, because the preview
  names entries that do not exist yet.
  ** *The first login with candidates* shows each candidate by its name and its teams and roles, never by the pseudonym,
  beside the choice "none of these is me" (`binding: new`). A `NOT_BINDABLE` keeps the candidates and clears the choice;
  the person may go back to change the entry, and the next save asks for the candidates again.
  ** *The page of the own entry* offers "Yes, this is right" only while the confirmation is due: it is the primary act
  while nothing was changed, and it is refused (it stays focusable and says why) while changes are unsaved, because it
  would confirm the entry as the server holds it and not as the person sees it. Saving and confirming read the session
  again, so `directoryConfirmationDue` and the shell's notice go; the notice is an information notice and is not shown on
  the page itself. The mock profile `read-only` answers the own entry `403 FORBIDDEN`, which the page shows as a refusal.
  ** *The way in* is the first column of the lens *Catalogs*, which links the persons and the import under the five
  catalogs, and the page *Settings*, which links the own entry. The import page says that it needs the right and shows
  nothing else without it.
  ** *No event* concerns the persons: `catalog-changed` is not used on the pages of the directory, and the persons page reads
  again after its own acts only.
  The web app keeps one read of the session for this (`refreshSession`), marked "to be replaced by Token Sheriff
  `client-framework-aspects`" (ADR-0004).

For the owner: whether `POST` for creation and a `PUT` that only changes is right (4); whether a retired entry may be
reactivated (5); whether `x-catalog` may name several catalogs (7); whether a retired service counts for the effective
protection needs (8); whether the reconfirmation should be a gate, as AUN-DIR-3 reads (13); whether the erasure should
ask for step-up; whether a user with an entry may have several memberships in the web app (14); whether `/directory/*`
and the route ids `catalogs.import` and `catalogs.persons` are the wanted home of the administration (1); whether
the status `409` for `BINDING_CANDIDATES` and `NOT_BINDABLE` is wanted; whether the phone's top bar should show
a link to the parent of the current page; whether the persons search should be an address query; whether a user with no
right to administer the catalogs may change their own entry, which the mock profile `read-only` refuses; and whether the
person who confirms an entry with unsaved changes should confirm the changed entry instead of being asked to save.

[#_decided_samm]
*Decided provisionally* with the SAMM completion (UC-SAMM-1 to UC-SAMM-5), so that no screen is blocked; the owner may
change each one. The bodies and addresses are in <<_samm_requests>>:

. *The addresses.* The lens *SAMM* gains `/samm/new` (`samm.new`, a new scope) and `/samm/{scope}/new`
  (`samm.assessment.new`, a new assessment), each before the route it would collide with. The segment `new` is reserved
  as a scope and as an assessment; so are `models` and `scopes` as scopes, which are paths below `/api/v1/samm/`.
. *The creates are requests of their own,* `POST /api/v1/samm/scopes` and `POST /api/v1/samm/{scope}/assessments`, because
  a scope names a subject and an assessment names its scope, and the server derives the address segment of both. The
  generic `POST /api/v1/documents/{repository}` would need the web app to know the layout of the two document kinds.
  The server assigns the address segment (a lower-case slug of the title, a number appended on a collision; a slug that is
  a reserved segment, `new`, or `models` and `scopes` for a scope, counts as a collision, so a scope titled "New" gets
  `new-2`) and the document id.
. *The scope is a document without a workflow.* Its targets are saved with the generic save of a document, the part
  `targets`, so the conflict, the validation and the step-up of the document controller serve it; there is no draft, no
  submit, no release and no `DOCUMENT_RELEASED`. The scope read gains `document` and `parts`.
. *The title and the subject of a scope cannot be changed* after the create; only the targets can. A scope of another
  subject is a new scope. A subject may have several scopes: the register does not forbid it.
. *A subject is written as the reference* `team:<id>`, `service:<id>` or `organisation` (AUN-SAMM-2) and read as
  `{kind, id, name}`.
. *A target is 0 to 3 in steps of 0.5,* with an optional due date; a practice without an entry has no target and no gap.
  AUN-SAMM-6 says "in steps of the model"; the model has no step, and 0.5 is the step of the targets of the mock (1.5,
  2, 2.5). The targets save sends the practices that changed, each as a whole entry, and `null` removes one.
. *The creator is always an assessor,* the first one; `assessors` names the further ones, none to 30. AUN-SAMM-1 says
  "assessors (pseudonyms)" without a minimum, and a first user of an empty installation has nobody to pick.
. *At most one assessment of a scope is open* (`draft` or `in-review`); a second one is refused with `409
  ASSESSMENT_OPEN` that names the open one. The scope list shows one open assessment per scope already.
. *A new assessment starts empty:* no answer is copied from an earlier one. The form of a new assessment prefills the model
  version of the assessment it was started from. Copying answers is a later decision.
. *Evidence belongs to the practice,* like the answers (the change `change-evidence-per-practice`): the read has
  `evidence` per practice, the practice's one file `answers/<practice-id>.yaml` and its blob cover answers, comments and
  evidence (no separate `evidence/<practice-id>.yaml`), and the save carries the whole list
  under the key `evidence`. A link has an optional `question`, a title and an absolute `https` address; a practice holds at
  most 50. `http`, relative and `file:` addresses are refused with `PATTERN`, there is no upload (AUN-SAMM-7).
. *The comment of an answer is AsciiDoc* of at most 8,000 characters, previewed by the server like every AsciiDoc field
  (`POST /api/v1/render/asciidoc`); the existing plain comments are valid AsciiDoc.
. *The pointers of a SAMM refusal* are those of the assessment as the SAMM read shows it
  (`/answers/<practice>/<question>/answer`, `/evidence/<practice>/<n>/<member>`), as the existing conflicts already are;
  the generic form keeps pointers into the value of a part. The scope's refusals are into the targets part
  (`/<practice>/target`).
. *A frozen assessment* is a released one: every write (draft, save, submit, release) answers `409 DOCUMENT_RELEASED` with
  the tag, the draft included, and the sheets are read-only. The way on is *Start a new assessment*, a link to
  `/samm/{scope}/new`; there is no request that copies or reopens a released assessment.
. *`MODEL_VERSIONS_DIFFER` is `422`* and names both assessments with their versions. AUN-SAMM-5 names the outcome, not the
  status. The comparison accepts an assessment in any state, as the first SAMM lens already uses it (an open assessment
  against the last release), reading the answers as saved.
. *The trend is one request per scope,* `GET /api/v1/samm/{scope}/trend`: released assessments of one model version, oldest
  first, with the overall, the function and the practice scores, and the number of the others (`omitted`). UC-SAMM-5 asks
  for a trend of several scopes as well; that is a report of the lens *Reports*, `GET /api/v1/reports/samm-trend` (<<_decided_reports>>). The scope sheet's chart
  reads this request instead of the assessments of the scope.
. *The pinned models are a list,* `GET /api/v1/samm/models`, with source, checksum, date and a default; pinning is not
  a web request (UC-SAMM-7 is configuration). The checksum is shown nowhere in the web app yet. The pickers offer only a
  pinned version; with none, the web app offers no new scope and no new assessment and says why.
. *The rights:* creating a scope, saving its targets and creating an assessment need `edit` on `samm-assessments`; reading
  needs `read`. Without `edit` the acts are shown disabled with the reason, and the requests answer `403 FORBIDDEN`.
. *The SAMM schemas are not served to the form kit:* the forms of a scope and an assessment are written for the SAMM lens
  (a subject picker, a targets table, an assessor picker), and the register fixes their limits. The scope and the
  assessment are not offered by `GET /api/v1/categories/{category}/schema`.
. *The mock answers the creates with fixed ids* (`mobile`, `2026-q4` of `shop`, `2026-h2` of `organisation`) and by the path of the scope: a scope that
  has an open assessment answers `ASSESSMENT_OPEN`, a scope that has none answers `CREATED`; the e2e flows type the same
  values.

Decided provisionally with the web app of the SAMM completion (the screens that use the requests above), each a reading
of the register that a screen needs and the register leaves open:

[start=20]
. *The form of a new assessment takes the model version from the scope.* The address `/samm/{scope}/new` admits no query
  (point 1), so a frozen assessment cannot name itself. The form preselects the model version of the scope's newest
  assessment when it is still pinned, else the scope's own version, else the default (point 9 and the section _A Frozen
  Assessment_ keep their wording: "the assessment it was started from" is the newest one).
. *The targets of a scope are kept on the page until they are saved for everyone.* A scope has no draft (point 3), so the
  sheet of the scope does not keep the changes as the person's draft; a change not yet saved is lost when the page is
  left, and leaving asks first (the question every new object asks). The document controller serves such a part with the
  option `drafts: false`.
. *A due day belongs to a target.* The editor offers the day only for a practice that has a target; "no target" removes the
  entry and with it the day. The empty day is no day; it is never sent as `""`.
. *The scope sheet shows the trend from two points.* One released assessment has no line to draw; the sheet then shows no
  trend. A trend that omits released assessments of other model versions says how many, also when what is left is too
  little for a line.
. *A link of the evidence leads out of the app only when it is absolute `https`.* The table draws such a link as an anchor
  (`rel="noopener noreferrer"`, a new tab) and every other value, which the server refuses on a save and which can
  only come from a hand-edited repository, as text. The editor refuses an address that is not `https` before it keeps a
  draft (the server's `PATTERN` stays the verdict).
. *The question of a link names a question of the practice or none,* chosen in the editor of the link from the practice's
  questions in order; a conflict of the evidence is decided for the list as a whole (the one field
  `/evidence/<practice>`).
. *The act that starts an assessment stands at three places:* the head of the scope sheet (disabled with the reason while
  one is open, no model is pinned or the user lacks `edit`), the status of a frozen practice sheet and the head of a
  released assessment, both only for a user who holds `edit`.
. *A step-up that the save of the targets asks for cannot complete yet.* The step-up of Token Sheriff leaves the page and
  returns to it, and a scope sheet has no draft (21) and no session storage that would carry the changed targets across
  that round trip. The page therefore stays, keeps the targets and says so (`samm.targets.step-up-stays`); the save
  succeeds only where the installation asks for no confirmation. It completes once a draft or a session storage is chosen
  for the roadmap.
. *What a dialog was opened on is checked when it is applied.* The evidence list may be replaced by a live refresh while
  the dialog of a link, or the question before a removal, is open. Applying an edit or a removal finds the link it was
  opened on again (same title, address and question); an edit that cannot find it, or finds it elsewhere, applies nothing
  the first time, says so in the dialog and keeps the typed values, and a second apply goes through (a link that is gone
  becomes a new link); a removal removes the confirmed link wherever it stands, and nothing when it is gone. A link added
  when the list reached 50 meanwhile is refused in the dialog with the limit.
. *A half-typed due day counts as unsaved input.* It is no change (it is not part of the targets), but leaving the page
  asks, the save state reads "changed" and the save waits with the reason until the day is finished or cleared.
. *A new scope with targets waits for the practices of its chosen model version.* Until they are read, the act says why;
  a scope without targets is not held back, and a version without a model file clears the targets as before.

For the owner: whether the scope and the assessment should be created through the generic `POST
/api/v1/documents/samm-assessments` with a schema of the two kinds served to the form kit (2, 18); whether the title and
the subject of a scope may change (4) and whether a subject may have several scopes (4); the step of a target and
whether the targets of a scope must be complete (6); whether the creator of an assessment is always an assessor (7);
whether more than one assessment of a scope may be open at once (8); whether a new assessment should offer to start from
the answers of the last released one (9); whether the evidence shares the file of its practice with the answers (10); the limits of the evidence and the comment (10, 11); the status `422` of
`MODEL_VERSIONS_DIFFER` (14); whether the trend may mix model versions with a marker instead of leaving the others out
(15); and whether the web app should pin a model version (16). With the screens (20 to 27): whether a new assessment
should take its model version from the newest assessment (20); whether the roadmap of a scope should keep a draft, so that
unsaved targets survive leaving the page (21); whether a due day may stand without a target (22); whether the trend should
draw a single released assessment (23); and whether the evidence of a hand-edited repository that holds a non-`https` address
should be drawn as text (24); and which draft or session storage the roadmap should use, because a step-up requested on the
save of the targets cannot complete until one is chosen (27; found in the design review: the scope sheet has no draft, so
the page stays and shows `samm.targets.step-up-stays`). Also: whether a refreshed list should instead interrupt a dialog
that is open on it (28).

[#_decided_reports]
*Decided provisionally* with the Reports lens (UC-SAMM-5, UC-TM-4, UC-ISMS-3), so that no screen is blocked; the owner may
change each one. The bodies and addresses are in <<_reports_requests>>:

. *The addresses.* The lens *Reports* gains `/reports/samm-trend` (query `scope`), `/reports/samm-compare` (`scope`, `from`,
  `to`), `/reports/threat-evaluation` and `/reports/risk-register` (`as-of`), each before the generic `/reports/{report}`,
  which stays and means "no such report". The names `scope`, `from`, `to` and `as-of` are new in `queries` of
  `canonical-addresses.json`; `isms.risk` gains `at`.
. *The reports live below `/api/v1/reports/`,* each with the right of the repository it reads (`read` on `samm-assessments`,
  `threat-models`; the register keeps its specified `GET /api/v1/isms/risks?as-of=`). The report of the threat models is
  not below `/api/v1/threat-models/` so that every report has one prefix; the lens *Threat models* reads the same request.
. *One summary for the dashboard,* `GET /api/v1/reports/summary`, so that `/reports` is one request and a card never
  disagrees with its report. A section is absent for a repository the user may not read; the request does not fail for
  it. The windows of "due soon" are 30 days.
. *The trend of every scope is one request,* `GET /api/v1/reports/samm-trend`, the points of
  `GET /api/v1/samm/{scope}/trend` without the practices, scope by scope. The page does not make one request per scope,
  and the scope sheet keeps the request of its own scope. Scopes of different model versions are drawn on one scale with a
  notice that names the versions; the versions are not mixed inside a scope (decision 15 of the SAMM completion).
. *The comparison page adds no request.* It reads the scope and `GET /api/v1/samm/{scope}/compare`; UC-SAMM-5 compares two
  assessments of one scope, so there is no comparison of two scopes. The `200` body of the comparison is now written down.
  `delta` is `to` minus `from`; the order of the two is the caller's.
. *The charts follow the series rule of the design.* The five business functions are the series 1 to 5 in model order
  (the page of one scope); the scopes are the series 1 to 5 in the order of the scope list (the overview), and a sixth and
  later scope is in the table and not in the chart, with a line that says so. Every chart carries its figures as direct
  labels and as a table. This is the decision of the screens and fixes no body.
. *The threat evaluation is one row per threat model,* with the service named in it, and a second list for the services
  that have no model (`withoutModel`; a retired service is in neither, a planned one is). The interval comes from the
  highest effective protection need (`P6M` very high, `P12M` high, `P24M` normal, AUN-TM-3); the review of a model that
  was never released is `unreleased`, not overdue.
. *"Open" and "untreated" threats* are counted as the threat model defines them (<<_decided_threat_models>>, point 6): `open` is the status
  `open`, `untreated` is open without a countermeasure and not transferred, `transferred` carries a reference into the
  risk register. AUN-TM-4 says "open threats" and "untreated threats" without a definition.
. *The risk register as of a date is answered with a summary of each entry,* not the entry: the running number, title,
  rating, treatment, owning team, version, release time, review date and the reference of its origin. The fields of a
  risk are fixed with the ISMS lens; the list needs these and no more. The list is not paged.
. *`as-of` is a calendar date or a tag name.* A value of the form `YYYY-MM-DD` is a date (UTC), every other value a tag. A
  date after today is `FUTURE`, a name that is no tag of `main` `UNKNOWN_TAG` (a tag into the collection branch does not count: the register as of a date reads released entries only), a date that does not exist or an unusable name
  `PATTERN`, each `422` with the pointer `/as-of`. The page keeps what was typed and says so beside the field.
. *The answer says what it read.* `asOf` carries the commit and its time, so that "as of 1 March" shows that the commit was
  that of 27 February; the list of tags is part of the answer and may be empty.
. *An entry of the as-of list opens as it was then:* `/isms/risks/<entry>?at=v<n>` for every entry of a read with `as-of`
  (also for the release that was the newest then, which a later release would otherwise replace), in the generic read of the
  document with `at` (<<_decided_lifecycle>>); the register as of now carries no `at`.
. *The register as of now is served from the index; a past commit that is not indexed answers `503 GIT_UNAVAILABLE`
  while the Git server is down.* The page then keeps the picker and offers the register as of now.
. *A report is read-only and has no export.* There is no PDF, spreadsheet or print layout; the table of each report is
  the machine-readable form.
. *The screens of the reports,* decided with the web app (no body changes): the breadcrumb is the way back, there is no
  link of its own. The trend of every scope draws the scopes that have a released assessment among the first five of the
  list, each in the colour and shape of its place in the list, so a scope keeps its colour whether or not an earlier one has
  points; a scope without a point is a row of the table that says so. The query `scope` of the trend that names no scope
  is a notice and every scope; the comparison with a `scope` that does not exist is the not-found view. The comparison
  offers its pairs as the neighbouring released assessments of the scope (oldest first), and two pickers over every
  assessment of the scope in any state; the same assessment twice is refused on the page, not by a request. The `as-of`
  field takes a day or a tag name, is checked against the pattern of the address in the browser and otherwise left to the
  server's `422`; the tags to choose from are those of the last answer, or of the register as of now when the first read was
  refused (a `422` or a `503`). A refusal stands beside the field only while the field holds the value that was refused;
  a tag is offered with the UTC day of its commit, the day the register itself names, and a tag whose commit holds no
  released risk says so for the tag and not for the end of a day. Show on the value the page already shows reads the register
  again. A review of the risk register counts as overdue or due soon (30 days) only when the register is read as of now.
  The threat evaluation can be ordered on the page (review due as served, service name, open, untreated threats) without a
  request; a user without `read` on `threat-models` meets a notice, not an empty table.

For the owner: whether the reports should be requests of the domain (`/api/v1/samm/...`, `/api/v1/threat-models/...`)
instead of one prefix (2); whether scopes of different model versions may share a chart (4); whether the comparison of two
scopes is wanted (5); the definition of an untreated threat and the thresholds of "due soon" (3, 7, 8); whether the register
as of a date should list the draft entries too, or the entry itself instead of its summary (9); whether the web app
should create reporting tags (10, 11) and whether the register as of two dates should be compared; whether reports should
be exported (14); whether a scope keeps its series colour by its place in the list (15, as decided) or only among the scopes that have points.


[#_decided_threat_models]
*Decided provisionally* with the threat models (UC-TM-1, UC-TM-2, UC-TM-4), so that no screen is blocked; the owner may
change each one. The bodies and addresses are in <<_threat_model_requests>>:

. *The addresses.* The lens *Threat models* gains `/threat-models/{service}/new` (`threat-models.new`, the recipe wizard) with
  the allowed query `recipe`, before `threat-models.model`; `new` is reserved as a model. `threat-models.model` admits `at`. The
  name `recipe` is new in `queries` of `canonical-addresses.json`.
. *The first column is the evaluation, the second the service.* The lens reads `GET /api/v1/reports/threat-evaluation` for the
  services (the table of the report, so one component serves both, and the services without a model with it) and
  `GET /api/v1/threat-models/{service}` for the models of one service, the intervals and the person's recipe drafts. A model
  is read by its address with `GET /api/v1/threat-models/{service}/{model}`, which is the generic read of the document with
  the members `service`, `model`, `title`, `address`, `review`, `threats` and `diagram`; the lifecycle (save, draft, submit,
  release, revisions, diff) is the generic requests of the repository `threat-models`. A domain request exists only where the
  address does not name the document.
. *The document type* is `threat-model` of the repository `threat-models`, collection `models`, with two files (the schema's
  `gdr-split` on `model`): `document.yaml` (the part `data`) and `model.yaml` (the part `model`). The workflow is the one of
  `test-note` with the transitions `threat-model.submit` and `threat-model.release` and the tag `models/<id>/v<n>`; the type is
  released more than once, so the first save after a release starts the next version in `draft` (UC-TM-2).
. *The format is Open Threat Model as YAML* (`model.yaml`), the subset of <<_threat_model_data>>; it closes the first two open
  questions of the concept (OTM or Threat Dragon JSON; how the model file fits the layout) for this step. Threat Dragon v2.6.2
  exports OTM but rejects an OTM import (concept, _Existing Tools_), so a team that edits in Threat Dragon cannot read the file
  back; the application is not a diagram editor. `otmVersion` is written by the server (`0.2.0`); the title of the model is
  `project.name` and the address segment is `project.id`, derived once at the creation and never changed (a renamed model keeps
  its address).
. *The treatment of a threat is kept on the threat,* in its `attributes` (`status`, `transferred`), and a countermeasure names its
  threat in its `attributes` (`threat`, `status`, `issues`), where OTM would put the state and the mitigations on the instance of
  a threat on a component or a data flow. Audit Notary evaluates a threat once and edits it in a table, not per component. The
  OTM README describes `attributes` as a map of strings, the published JSON Schema (`otm_schema.json`) allows any object; the
  lists (`issues`) rely on the schema. A countermeasure belongs to one threat. A tool that reads OTM instances finds none.
. *The status of a threat is `open`, `mitigated` or `accepted`.* `untreated` is an open threat that no countermeasure names and
  that carries no `transferred`; `transferred` is a threat that carries it, whatever its status. The ids are `T-<nn>` and
  `M-<nn>`. The member `transferred` (the reference `isms-register:risk/<id>`) is written by the transfer of UC-TM-3, a later step;
  the sheet of this step shows it and writes nothing to it. This is the definition the Reports decision (<<_decided_reports>>, point 8) waited for.
. *The diagram is generated* by the server from `trustZones`, `components` and `dataflows` and served as the member `diagram` of a
  read, a vector image as a data URL (`image/svg+xml`), never stored (AUN-TM-2: diagrams are data, images are generated). The
  layout (zones side by side, components stacked in their zone, flows as arrows) is the server's; the file holds no
  coordinates. A model without a component has `"diagram": null`.
. *A first save of a model is a request of its own,* `POST /api/v1/threat-models/{service}`: the service is the path, the server
  writes `service`, `recipe` and `otmVersion` and derives the address segment from `/project/name` (a collision gets a number,
  `new` counts as a collision). Only `/project/name` is mandatory in a first save; a model is saved incomplete and the
  transition asks for the rest, as every document. The generic `POST /api/v1/documents/threat-models` would make the web app
  derive the segment and write the service reference.
. *Lists are saved as a whole,* `/threats`, `/mitigations`, `/components`, `/dataflows`, `/trustZones`, `/project`, and a conflict is
  decided per list. The countermeasure editor sends `/threats` and `/mitigations` in one save. A key per element (a pointer
  `/threats/T-03`) would give a conflict per threat and needs an addition to the pointer rule of the generic save; it is not
  decided here.
. *The review date is set by the act of starting the review,* which prefills `/last-review` with `review.today`; an edit of a
  released model does not change it. The due date counts from the `last-review` of the last release
  (<<_decided_reports>>, point 7), and a review date that is typed and not yet released shows its due date from
  `reviewIntervals`. The intervals are data of the kind and are served in the service page and the model read; there is no
  request of their own.
. *The bounds of the schema:* likelihood, impact and risk reduction are whole numbers from 0 to 100 (OTM fixes the range only for
  `riskReduction`; its example uses 0 to 100 for the others); texts at most 200 and 2,000 characters; at most 20 trust zones,
  200 components, 400 data flows, 200 threats and 400 countermeasures, 20 issues of a countermeasure; `type` of a component is
  `external-entity`, `process` or `data-store`; the categories of a threat are the six of STRIDE. The server checks that a
  countermeasure names a threat of the model; it does not check that a component's trust zone or a data flow's ends exist
  (the form kit has no in-file reference picker, so the ids are typed).
. *The recipes are read below the category,* `GET /api/v1/categories/threat-models/recipes` and `…/recipes/{recipe}`, the REST form
  of `read_recipe` (a category and an optional id), with the optional `lang` (`en` or `de`, English without it). A recipe file has
  `title`, `description`, steps with `id`, `title`, `guidance`, `fills`; every text is a string or a map of language to text, and the
  request answers the text of `lang`. This extends AUN-TM-5, which fixes plain strings. Two recipes ship in the mock, `quick-start`
  and `stride`; whether a recipe is a document itself stays the concept's open question.
. *A recipe draft is the draft of a model that does not exist yet.* One per person, service and recipe, replaced as a whole by
  `PUT /api/v1/threat-models/{service}/drafts/{recipe}`, read by the matching `GET`, ended by the create. The library keys a draft
  by a document that exists; a draft of a document to come needs the library to key it by the service and the recipe, which is
  open. A draft with field errors is refused like every draft (<<_decided_lifecycle>>, point 10), so a step is left only when it
  is valid; the create is the last step and carries the `owner` and the `last-review` (prefilled with today; the wizard does not
  prefill the owner, point 31).
. *The rights:* reading the lens and the evaluation needs `read` on `threat-models`, creating, saving and submitting `edit`, the
  release `approve`. The mock gives the user of the profile `workshop` the right `approve` on `threat-models` (it had `read` and
  `edit`), so that the release of a review can be walked through; `release` keeps no right on the repository. Without `edit`
  the acts are shown disabled with the reason, and the requests answer `403 FORBIDDEN`.
. *A service may have several models,* each with its own row in the evaluation and its own address; a retired service keeps its
  models readable and gets no new one (`409 SERVICE_RETIRED`). A service that is planned or deprecated can have a model.
. *References and the search.* A reference `threat-models:threat-model/<document>#<threat id>` resolves to the title
  `<model title>: <threat id>` (for example "Payment API, checkout flow: T-07", the title of the reference of the risk R-009) and the
  address of the model; the search lists the models with the category `threat-models` and the model's address.
. *The recipe is a `WizardPage`* at `/threat-models/{service}/new?recipe=`: one page per step (the guidance above, the form of the
  step below), a last page that checks and asks for the owner and the review date, and the act *Save*; the sub-form is built from the
  schema at `fills`. The web app shows the recipes to choose from on the page of a service that has none or wants another, and
  the recipes a person has started as *continue*. (Decided later, point 27: the wizard is a sheet of the lens, not a `WizardPage`.)

. *A save keeps the stored `transferred`.* `attributes.transferred` is `readOnly` in the schema and the server takes it from the
  stored file by the `id` of the threat, ignoring a value in the sent `/threats` list (a refusal code would add a request outcome
  the form kit has no text for); the transfer of UC-TM-3 is the only writer. A threat added by a save carries none.
. *The issues of a countermeasure cannot be edited in the form kit's table.* `mitigations` is a `DataTable` and rule 13 of the form kit
  (`web-app.adoc`) keeps an array inside a row read-only. The sheet of the lens does not use that table for the countermeasures:
  its own editor (`CountermeasureEditor`, decision 21) takes the issue ids as one field, separated by commas, and an agent enters
  them through the REST API as well (AUN-TM-4). Left open: whether the form kit should get a control for a list of short texts, so
  that the generic form and the lens read alike. The bounds stay `maxItems` 20, unique, 1 to 64 characters. The editor keeps the
  stored ids as they are while the issue field is left alone, so an id that holds a space or a comma (the schema allows one)
  survives an edit of another field; once the person changes the text of the field, it is read as ids separated by commas,
  semicolons or white space, and an id that holds a separator cannot be entered through the form (the REST API can).

. *The sheet of a model has two parts and so two document controllers* (decided with the screens of the lens). The data of the document (`owner`, `last-review`) and the
  file of the model (`model`) each have their own draft, blob id and conflicts, and *Save for everyone* sends one save for each
  part that has changes, in that order (`model`, then `data`): two signed commits, not one, although the body of the generic
  save could carry both parts. A refusal of one part keeps the changes of the other. Left open: one commit for both parts needs
  the controller to key its changes by part, which the shared controller does not do yet.
. *The countermeasure editor and the threat table are the lens's own components,* not the form kit's `DataTable` through
  `SchemaForm`: a countermeasure names one threat by a choice among the threats of the model, a threat shows its status in its row
  and its transfer as a link, and the editor edits the issue ids (decision 19). The rest of the file (project, trust zones,
  components, data flows) is the form kit's `SchemaForm` over the schema of the property `model` without `otmVersion`,
  `threats` and `mitigations`.
. *Removing a threat removes its countermeasures in the same save,* and the dialog says so: the file never holds a
  countermeasure of a threat that is gone (the server answers `REFERENCE_NOT_FOUND` otherwise). The ids `T-<nn>` and `M-<nn>`
  are the next free number after the highest taken, so the id of a removed entry is not reused while a later one stands.
. *The labels of the form are catalogue keys relative to the value of the part:* `category.threat-models.threat-model.field.<path>`
  with `<path>` as in the schema of the property `model` (`threats`, `project.name`) or of the document (`owner`, `last-review`).
  The pointer of a `MANDATORY_FIELDS_MISSING` names the field in the document (`/model/project/name`); the sheet strips the
  leading `/model` to find its label and its field. The app's own catalogue carries the keys, because the server's catalogue of
  the document type is not served yet.
. *The act *Start the review* is offered to everyone who may edit,* in every state, and is disabled with its reason when the review
  date is already today; it sets `/last-review` of the part `data` to `review.today` and nothing else. The due date a typed review
  date would give is shown as a sentence from `reviewIntervals` and the highest protection need (`review.protectionNeed`).
. *The release page reads the lists change by change.* The server's diff carries the whole lists `/threats` and `/mitigations`; the
  page makes one change of each entry that is new, gone or changed, by its `id` (`/threats/T-03`), so the approver reads a
  threat, not two lists of nine. A change of any other property is the generic one.
. *The first column is the table of the report;* a service without a model is offered *New threat model* in its row for a person
  with `edit`, and `/threat-models/{service}/new` is routed to a placeholder inside the lens until the recipe wizard is built
  (it is the recipe wizard now, points 27 to 34).
. *The wizard is a sheet in the last column of the lens,* not a `WizardPage` of its own (this replaces the page of point 17): the
  person keeps the evaluation and the service beside the steps, as with a new scope. `/threat-models/{service}/new` without
  `recipe` is the choice of the recipe (`GET …/recipes?lang=`, with the position of a started recipe from `drafts` of the service
  page); with `recipe` it is the wizard of that recipe. A recipe the server does not know (`404`) is told on the page with the way
  to the choice, and there is no wizard without a recipe (a model without one is for the REST API and an agent). A person without
  `edit` and a retired service get no wizard and are told why.
. *The steps.* A recipe of `n` steps has `n + 1` pages: the steps in the order of the recipe, then *Review and create* at position
  `n` (the `step` of a draft, zero-based, is the page the person goes on to). The texts of the steps are read with the `lang` of
  the interface; a change of language reads them again and keeps what was filled in.
. *The form of a step* is cut from the schema of `GET /api/v1/categories/threat-models/schema` at `fills` (an object or a table of
  objects of the model file, with the `readOnly` members left out, such as `project.id`, which the server writes); `/threats` and
  `/mitigations` are the table of the threats and the editor of the countermeasures of the sheet of a model, which send the whole
  list. A `fills` that names no member of the file is told on the step, which can be passed.
. *When the draft is written.* `PUT …/drafts/{recipe}` goes when the person goes on from a step (the whole draft, `step` the page
  they go to, the part `data` once they have been on the last page), not on a change and not on going back. The wizard stays on
  the step when the server refuses the draft (`422`) and marks the fields; for a refusal of a field of another step it goes to
  that step. Leaving the page with changes that were not written asks first (`LeaveGuard`).
. *The last page* lists every step with its name or the number of its entries and an act to change it, and asks for the `owner`
  and the `last-review`. The review date starts as `today` of the service page; the owner is not prefilled, because the service
  page names a team and the catalog of roles is not related to the service (this replaces the prefill of point 13). Only the name
  of the project (`/project/name`) is required before the create.
. *The create* is `POST /api/v1/threat-models/{service}` with `recipe` and both parts, sent through the shared step-up handler
  (the register lists no confirmation for it, so it asks nothing in the mock). A `422` names fields: the wizard goes to the step
  that fills the first one of the part `model`, and stays on the last page for the part `data`. A `409 SERVICE_RETIRED` (a new
  outcome of the client: "This service is retired and gets no new threat model.") and the other refusals are told above the step
  and nothing is cleared. After the `201` the page moves to the address of the answer (replacing the wizard in the history), and
  the evaluation and the service page are read again.
. *Limits of the mock.* The draft routes exist for `report-scheduler` and `notification-gateway` only (README of `web-mock`), so
  the wizard of another service fails at the first step with a refused write; the create of `report-scheduler` is the only one
  that leads to a readable model.
. *A new piece of the closed vocabulary, `Stepper`* (the steps as a numbered list, the reached ones as acts that go back, the
  current one marked; on a phone the count and the current step). It is the only component the wizard adds.

For the owner: whether OTM as YAML is the format (4) and whether the treatment may live in `attributes` (5), given that the OTM
README calls `attributes` a map of strings and that a threat has one status, not one per component; whether a countermeasure may
belong to one threat only (5); the definition of `untreated` and `transferred` (6); whether the diagram is generated from the file
(7) and which tool draws it; whether a model needs a request of its own for the first save (8); whether a conflict per list is
fine (9); whether the review date is the act of the person who starts the review (10); the bounds (11); whether recipe texts may
be per language, which changes AUN-TM-5 (12); whether a recipe draft may be a draft of a model that does not exist (13), which
the library does not offer; whether the person who edits may release in the mock (14); whether a service may have several
models (15); whether a save keeps the stored `transferred` or refuses a changed one (18); how a person enters the issues of a
countermeasure (19). With the screens of the lens: whether the two parts of the document should be one commit (20); whether the threat table and the
countermeasure editor should be the form kit's (21), and whether removing a threat should take its countermeasures with it (22); whether the
start of a review should be offered in every state (24). With the recipe wizard: whether it is a sheet in the lens and not a
`WizardPage` (27), whether the owner should be prefilled by the server (31), and whether the create should ask for a
confirmation (32).

[#_decided_incidents]
*Decided provisionally* with the incident reports (UC-IR-1, UC-IR-2, UC-IR-3), so that no screen is blocked; the owner may
change each one. The bodies and addresses are in <<_incident_requests>>:

. *The stages are parts, and the field paths of the deadlines move into them.* AUN-IR-1 to AUN-IR-3 still describe one
  `document.yaml` with `aware-at`, `personal-data-affected` and `nis2-significant` at its root; the concept defers the
  change that moves them into the parts. This step takes it provisionally: `title` stays in `document.yaml` (the part
  `data`), every other field lives in the part of its stage (`/triage/aware-at`, `/notifications/supervisory-authority`),
  and the six deadline rules read those paths (<<_incident_deadlines>>). The specification (AUN-IR-1 to AUN-IR-3) is not
  changed by this step; it follows when the owner accepts the layout.
. *The addresses.* The lens *Incidents* gains `/incidents/{year}/new` (`incidents.new`) before `incidents.incident`; `new` is
  reserved as an incident; `incidents.incident` admits `at` and `stage`; the placeholder pattern `incident` is case sensitive
  and refuses `new`. The name `stage` is new in `queries` of `canonical-addresses.json`. A person who returns from the
  confirmation of a stage lands on the same tab.
. *Three domain requests and the generic lifecycle.* The years (`GET /api/v1/incidents`), the incidents of a year with their
  stage statuses and deadlines (`GET /api/v1/incidents/{year}`) and an incident by its address, with `stages`, `deadlines` and
  `protection` (`GET /api/v1/incidents/{year}/{incident}`), because the address names the year and the incident, never the
  repository, and the index of the lens needs figures the generic list does not carry. The lifecycle (create, save, draft,
  submit, release, revisions, diff) is the generic requests of the repository `isms-incidents-<year>`; only the completion
  and the reopening of a stage and the report are requests of their own.
. *One kind, one repository per year.* The schema answer names `isms-incidents-<year>` as the repository of the kind; the
  web app learns the repositories from `GET /api/v1/incidents`. This does not close the open question of the library
  (a kind is bound to one repository alias, GDR-FORMS-1).
. *The id of an incident is a ULID,* as of every document, and is the address segment; the earlier example `inc-0007` was a
  placeholder. There is no incident number. A readable label is the `title`.
. *A stage is a part with its own `completion`.* `completion` is `{at, by}` in the value of the part, `by` a pseudonym; it is
  `readOnly`. A save or a draft that touches it answers `422 VALIDATION` with the code `READ_ONLY`; a write into a
  completed stage answers `409 STAGE_LOCKED` (new outcome) and writes nothing. Both are the rules of the concept
  (<<_incident_save>>).
. *Completion and reopening are two requests,* `POST …/stages/{stage}/complete` and `…/reopen`, on the document of the
  generic lifecycle (the path is the one the step-up handler already lists). The answers are `COMPLETED` and `REOPENED` with
  the new blob id of the part and the statuses of the stages afterwards. The optional member `blob` makes a completion
  fail with the existing `CHANGED_SINCE_REVIEW` when the part was saved since the form loaded it.
. *A refused completion names both causes.* `422 STAGE_NOT_COMPLETABLE` (new outcome) with `missing` and `open`; it does not
  reuse `MANDATORY_FIELDS_MISSING`, because a completion can also fail for open predecessors alone. The new outcomes
  `STAGE_NOT_APPLICABLE` and `STAGE_STATE` (`409`) answer a stage that does not apply and a completion or reopening that is
  no act of the stage's status.
. *Submitting and releasing answer `STAGES_OPEN`* (new outcome, `422`) instead of `MANDATORY_FIELDS_MISSING`: the staged
  document's rule is that every applicable stage is completed, which includes its mandatory fields, so the refusal is the
  list of the applicable stages that are not completed, each with its missing fields. It satisfies AUN-IR-3 ("a list of the
  missing fields"), grouped by stage. A stage not applicable is never listed. The outcome `MANDATORY_FIELDS_MISSING` stays for
  the documents that have no stages.
. *Step-up asks for the completion of `review` only,* as the declaration of the concept says (`complete: {step-up: true}`);
  reopening a stage asks for none. The reasoning is the concept's: the review closes the file.
. *The right is `edit` to complete and reopen,* `approve` to release, on the repository of the year; the concept leaves a
  right of its own for a stage (for example the data protection officer for the assessment) open. The mock gives the user of
  the profile `workshop` the right `approve` on `isms-incidents-2026` and `read` on `isms-incidents-2025` (the year is
  released), so that the release of a review can be walked through; `release` keeps no right on the incident repositories.
. *The stages travel in the read.* The incident read carries, per stage, its declaration (`after`, `when` as data,
  `narrative`, `stepUp`), its derived `status`, `completion`, `missing` and `blockedBy`, the flag `predecessorReopened` (a
  completed stage that has an open stage among its ancestors) and `unanswered` (a stage that does not apply only because the
  field of its condition is empty), so that the tabs, their markers and the disabled *Complete* need no second request. The
  sentence of the condition is a catalogue message, not data.
. *A stage whose condition field is absent is `not-applicable`* (a boolean that is absent is false), with `unanswered: true`,
  so that the tab says the question is open and the stage "applies when …", and not that the stage was considered and does
  not apply.
. *The deadline rules are data with the grammar AUN-IR-2 describes,* and are held by the server: `when` is a field that is
  `true`, or, if it is no boolean, present; the due time is a time field plus a period; a deadline whose time field is empty
  is `OPEN` with `due: null`. The grammar is not offered by the library yet (GDR-FORMS-6 has three forms); the register
  fixes the answer, not the file syntax.
. *A deadline sent late stays `MISSED`.* `MET` needs `sent-at` not after `due`; `MISSED` is `asOf` strictly after `due`, or a
  late `sent-at`, and the row carries `sentAt` so that the page says it was sent late. AUN-IR-2 says a missed deadline stays
  visible; it does not say whether late sending changes the status.
. *The clock stops at the release.* A released incident and every revision compute `deadlines` at the instant of their
  release (a revision before the first release at its commit time), so that `report.adoc` is reproducible (AUN-IR-1); a
  deadline that was open then stays open in the record. The release page lists the deadlines that are open before the person
  confirms, because a released incident is frozen and the notification can no longer be recorded in it. The working state is
  computed at the time of the read, and the web app reads the incident again when it comes back to it:
  there is no event for the passing of a due time and no reminder (reminders are runtime state, not persistence).
. *The Due lens lists the deadlines that are still a duty.* One item of the kind `deadline` per `OPEN` deadline with a date
  and per `MISSED` deadline that was not sent at all, of an incident that is not released; a late-sent one is not listed. The
  item carries the new member `deadline` (`rule`, `status`), the address of the incident with `?stage=notifications`, and the
  due date in UTC. An incident in review is no item of the kind `review` in this step.
. *An incident is released once.* The document type is not released more than once (a threat model is): the release freezes
  it (`frozen`, `DOCUMENT_RELEASED` for every write), and a change after the release is a new incident that names the earlier
  one in the ticket or a risk link. Whether a corrected report may be released as the next version of the same incident is
  an owner question.
. *The protection needs are derived while the incident is open, and recorded at the release.* AUN-IR-1 lists
  `protection-needs` among the fields of the file; a stored copy of a working incident would drift from the catalog
  (AUN-DIR-5). The read carries the maximum per dimension over the effective needs of the affected services and, per
  dimension, the first service that decides it. The release writes the derived values and the titles of the affected services
  into the release commit (`protection.yaml`), and a released incident and its revisions read them from there: otherwise a
  later change of a protection need in the catalog would make a fresh generation differ from the committed `report.adoc`,
  which the CI check of AUN-IR-1 forbids. The alternative is to pin the revision of the catalog in the release.
. *The notifications are named members of the part `notifications`,* the names of AUN-IR-2 (`supervisory-authority`,
  `nis2-early-warning`, …), each `{sent-at, recipient, channel, reference}`, plus `data-subjects` (GDPR Art. 34, no deadline in
  the rules) and a list `others`; `nis2-intermediate-reports` is a list whose entries carry the `due` that the authority
  asked for. The channels are `reporting-portal`, `email`, `phone`, `post`, `other`. The recipient is a short text (an
  organisation or an office), not a catalog entry: the authorities are not in the catalog of external parties and the
  application connects to none of them. The table of the lens is these members.
. *The privacy assessment has the fields the concept names,* the data subjects by category with approximate numbers, the
  approximate number of records, the level of the risk (`unlikely`, `risk`, `high-risk`), the assessment as AsciiDoc and the
  two decisions, to notify the authority (Art. 33(1)) and to inform the data subjects (Art. 34(1)), plus an optional
  reason for a delay. The two decisions switch no deadline: see the owner question below.
. *The lists of values are provisional:* the severity (`low`, `medium`, `high`, `critical`), the kind of an action
  (`containment`, `recovery`, `other`), the category of a root cause (`configuration`, `software-defect`, `process`,
  `access-control`, `third-party`, `other`), the classification (`security-incident`, `personal-data-breach`). The bounds are
  those of the schema above.
. *The narrative is part of the part `review`.* The three AsciiDoc fragments of the review stage are joined by the server into
  its value, so that the form edits them as `x-asciidoc` strings and the stage's mandatory fields include them; their files
  stay `narrative/*.adoc`.
. *The first save needs only a title;* the address's year chooses the repository. A save of `/triage/aware-at` into another
  year than the repository's answers `422 VALIDATION` with the code `OUT_OF_YEAR` (new, the year of the awareness is the rule
  of AUN-IR, in the repository's name), so that the index of a year never holds an incident of another one; a report started
  on 2 January for an awareness of 31 December is written in the repository of the year before (the person needs `edit`
  there). There is no request that moves an incident to another repository.
. *The report is read, not downloaded:* `GET …/report` answers the AsciiDoc source of the generated `report.adoc`
  (`generated` for the working state, `committed` for a release), the web app renders it through the render request, and no
  PDF or export is part of the web app. The text the template writes is English. The web app renders it by level-2 section
  (the render request is limited to 65,536 bytes) and bounds the narrative fields at 8,000 characters.
. *The diff of a staged document is by part, then by section:* the generic diff, whose parts are the stages (`from` is `null`,
  every field an addition); the release view groups by the first segment of the pointer. A stage that is `not-applicable` is
  left out.
. *The labels are catalogue keys relative to the document:* `category.incident-reports.incident.field.<part>.<path>`
  (`triage.severity`, `notifications.supervisory-authority.sent-at`, `title`), the stage `category.incident-reports.stage.<id>`
  with `.guidance` and `.when`, a deadline `category.incident-reports.deadline.<rule>` with `.basis`. The app's own catalogue
  carries them, as for the threat models.
. *No new event.* A completion is a save: `document-saved` with `part` the stage and `by`; a submit and a release are
  `document-transitioned`; the creation is `document-created`.
. *The years are the repositories the user may read,* from `GET /api/v1/incidents`; `current` says whether the year of
  today has one. Without a right on any, the list is empty and not `403`. The lens then shows the empty state.
. *The new incident is a sheet of the lens* (the title and the triage form), not a page of its own: the person keeps the list
  of the year beside it; nothing is stored before the first save, so there is no draft of an incident that does not exist.
. *Reading while the Git server is unreachable* serves the years, the lists, the incidents and the reports from the last
  fetched state; the writes answer `503 GIT_UNAVAILABLE` and keep the draft, as everywhere.

*Decided provisionally* with the lens *Incidents*, part A (the web app of the years, the year, the sheet, the stages, the
clock, the notices and the report), so that no screen is blocked; the owner may change each one (the numbering goes on):

[start=32]
. *The labels of a stage's form are of the kind `incident.<part>`:* the key of a field is
  `category.incident-reports.incident.<part>.field.<path>` (`…incident.triage.field.severity`,
  `…incident.notifications.field.supervisory-authority.channel.option.email`), and the title is
  `…incident.data.field.title`. This replaces the pattern `category.incident-reports.incident.field.<part>.<path>` of point 27: each
  form edits the value of one part, so its pointers have no part in them, and a stage that names a field like another
  (`data-subjects` of the privacy assessment and of the notices) keeps its own words. The form kit needs no change.
. *The form of a stage leaves out `completion`,* which only *complete* and *reopen* write; the title (the part `data`) is a form
  of its own at the top of the triage tab, not a stage, and stays editable until the release.
. *A part that was never saved is saved with `"blob": ""`.* The register says nothing about the blob of a part that has none
  (`"blob": null` in the read); the web app sends the empty string, which the controller of the document already does for a
  part without a blob. A sign-off of such a part (`…/complete`) sends no `blob` at all.
. *A sign-off names the blob of the part as the person last saw it:* the blob of the read, or, right after the person's own
  save, the one that save answered, so that *Save for everyone, then Complete stage* does not meet `CHANGED_SINCE_REVIEW`. A
  `CHANGED_SINCE_REVIEW` answer to a completion is read as "someone saved the stage since you opened it": the incident is read
  again and nothing is signed off. `STAGE_STATE`, `STAGE_NOT_APPLICABLE` and `DOCUMENT_RELEASED` also read it again.
. *A completed stage is read-only until it is reopened,* a stage that does not apply stays editable (its data are kept, never
  checked), and a save that is refused with `STAGE_LOCKED` keeps the changes on the page, says why and reads the incident
  again, so that the stage shows as completed.
. *The act of a stage acts on the saved part,* like every step of a workflow: with changes only the person has, the bar offers
  *Save for everyone, then Complete stage*. The act is disabled, with the reason, while a stage before it is open or a conflict of
  the part is undecided. The stage that asks for step-up says in a line, in domain words, that the person confirms their identity
  once more. *Save for everyone* saves the parts that changed one after the other (the title first, then the stages in their
  order), each as its own signed commit; a conflict or a refusal of one part does not hold back the others, and a confirmation
  that was not answered stops the rest, which wait for the next press instead of asking again.
. *The tab is the query `stage`.* A change of tab replaces the address (`replace`, no entry of the history for each tab), and
  an address without a stage, or with one the incident does not declare, opens the first stage that is open and does not wait
  for another, then the first that is open, then the first that applies. That tab is found once, when the sheet opens: a
  completion, or a read that brings the work of someone else, never moves the person to another tab; only the query does.
. *The clock is told against the page's own time while the incident is worked on, and against `asOf` once it is frozen or a
  revision.* An `OPEN` deadline without a notice whose due time has passed on the page's clock reads *Missed* ("Overdue by …") and needs the
  person until the next read says so; a deadline that is missed and was never sent is a box, one that was sent late is
  information (a dot and the words "Sent …, … late"). The server's status stays the truth: the page never writes a status.
. *The notices are a table over the form.* The tab *Notifications* lists every named member that has a time, and every entry of
  `nis2-intermediate-reports` and `others`, with the deadline each meets (by the `metBy` pointer of the deadline); the form of the
  part stands under it as *Record a notice*. The form kit marks the inner mandatory fields of each member as required, so each
  member carries the hint that it is filled in only when the notice was sent.
. *The report is read on demand,* with the act *Show the report*; it is rendered by level-2 section, with the title of the report
  left out (it is the head of the sheet) and the headings one level lower, and the preview says that it is generated from the
  saved state (a release: committed) and that it is written in English whatever the language of the app.
. *A year or an incident the person may not read is the not-found view* (the register answers `404`); a `403 FORBIDDEN` that still
  arrives, because a right was withdrawn while the page was open, is a notice in place of the column.
. *`New incident` is offered on the year column* to a person with `edit` on the repository of the current year (`current: true`); the
  addresses `incidents.new` and `incidents.release` are built by part B (points 46 to 50).
. *The Due item of a deadline is worded by the web app:* the rule is read from the member `deadline`, and the title of the incident is
  the server's `title` without its ending `: <rule>`; a title that does not end so is shown as it is.
. *The history and the revisions carry the kinds `complete` and `reopen`* (with `stage`), as the mock does; the generic lines
  `create`, `save`, `submit` and `release` keep theirs, and a `save` of an incident names its `part`.

*Decided provisionally* with the lens *Incidents*, part B (the release of an incident with its diff, and the form of a new
incident), so that no screen is blocked; the owner may change each one (the numbering goes on):

[start=46]
. *The release lists the open deadlines and does not refuse for them.* Before the person confirms, the page lists every deadline
  that is still a duty (`OPEN`, or `MISSED` and never sent; one sent late is a fact, a met one is done), worded as the clock of the
  sheet words it, with the reminder that a released incident is frozen and a notice not recorded by then can no longer be recorded,
  and a link to the tab *Notifications*. The margin counts them. Refusing the release while a deadline is open is not decided (the
  owner question of point 16 stays); after the release the same list is told against the time of the release, under the sentence
  that the clock has stopped (when no deadline is still a duty, neither the list nor the sentence is drawn).
. *The release page shows the diff by stage, then by field.* The parts of the diff are the stages (the title is the part `data`,
  its group is *Title*); a group is one stage, its rows are the changes in the order of the form, each worded as the form words
  the field. An incident is released once, so `from` is always `null` and every change is a whole top-level field, which is why
  the first segment of the pointer is the row (point 29). The sign-off of a stage (`/completion`) is the row *Stage signed off*
  with the person's name, taken from the stage of the read when its time is the one of the change, else the time alone: the
  pseudonym is never shown. A released incident has no changes to release: its record is the release it was made from.
. *`STAGES_OPEN` is told, not forecast.* The page does not disable *Release* for an open stage (the server is the truth: a stage can
  be reopened while the page is open); its margin says which stages are open, and a refused release (`422 STAGES_OPEN`) is a notice
  that nothing was released with one link to the tab of every stage the server names; the incident is read again.
. *A create may ask for step-up.* `POST /api/v1/documents/{repository}` carries the member `password` like the save (the
  generalisation of <<_the_step_up_handler>>) and goes through the step-up handler, although the shipped workflows declare none for
  a create. The form asks for the title (mandatory) and the time the organisation became aware (optional, `OUT_OF_YEAR` is the text
  *This time is not in the year of the incident* at that field); a new incident is offered in the current year to a person with
  `edit`, and for another year the sheet says that a new incident is made in the current year. The new page lands on the incident
  with `replace`, and the year and the years are read again.
. *The mock has one release refusal for the stages and none for the deadlines.* The state `incident-release-stages-open` answers the
  release with `STAGES_OPEN` for the review (reopened meanwhile) and then reads with the review reopened; no incident of the mock is in review with an open deadline, so the
  list of point 46 is driven by the unit tests with an overriding answer, and the e2e tests intercept the read of the incident.
  The diff of the *Credential stuffing* holds the `completion` of the review, so in the base state, where the review is still open,
  the release page of the draft shows a sign-off that the read does not yet have (the page then says that the incident must be
  submitted first, and the diff is not released).

For the owner: whether the specification may follow the layout of point 1 (the stages as parts, the paths of the
deadlines in them, which moves AUN-IR-1 to AUN-IR-3 and the deadline inputs of the concept); whether a kind may be bound to
a repository per year (4); whether `STAGES_OPEN` may replace `MANDATORY_FIELDS_MISSING` for a staged document (9) and
whether the completion of a stage needs its own right (11); whether reopening a stage should ask for step-up, since it
undoes a sign-off (10); whether a deadline sent late should read `MISSED` (15) and whether the clock should stop at the
release, and whether a release should be refused while a deadline is open (16); whether the Due lens should list the deadlines as in point 17 and the incident in review; whether a corrected
report of a released incident is the next version of the same incident (18); whether the protection needs should be recorded
at the release, as decided, or the revision of the catalog pinned in it (19); the vocabularies of the points 20 to 22, above all the severity scale, which belongs to the incident
process of the organisation; whether the recipient of a notification should be a catalog entry (20); whether an assessment
that finds the breach unlikely to result in a risk (`notify-authority` is `false`) should switch the deadline `gdpr-art33` off,
as Art. 33(1) says the notification is not due then (21; the rules of AUN-IR-2 do not know it); whether the server should
refuse an awareness outside the year of the repository (24) or let an incident move between the repositories; whether a generated report may be in English only, and whether it
should be rendered by section or the limit of the render request raised for it (25); and
the two statements of <<_incident_sources>> that the tool of this change could not check against EUR-Lex (GDPR Art. 33,
NIS2 Art. 23(4)), which the owner should verify before the rules are called `SPECIFIED`.

For the owner, from part A of the lens: whether the server should send the title of the incident and the id of the rule as members of
the Due item, so that the web app need not strip the suffix of the title (point 44); whether the first save of a part without a
blob should send no `blob` instead of an empty one (point 34); whether the page may call an `OPEN` deadline *Missed* before the
server does (point 39), or should wait for the next read; and whether the form kit should mark the inner mandatory fields of
an optional section only once the section is started (the notices show every member's *Sent at*, *Recipient* and *Channel* as
required, which is true only after the notice is begun, point 40).

For the owner, from part B of the lens: whether a release should be refused while a deadline is open (point 46; the page lists them
and does not block); whether a create should be able to ask for step-up at all (point 48: it is wired, the shipped workflows ask for
none); and whether the stopped clock should be told after the release when no deadline was still a duty (point 46: the list and its
sentence are drawn only with rows, so a release with every deadline met or sent shows the seal alone). If a create may ask for
step-up, the multi-factor path leaves the page, and there is no draft of an incident that does not exist (point 31): the title and the
time typed so far are lost across the redirect. Decided provisionally: leave it as it is (the password path stays on the page, and the
shipped workflows ask for no step-up on a create); the options are a keep of its own (such as the session storage), a `beforeLeave` that
refuses the create under multi-factor with a notice, or leaving the loss.

[#_decided_isms]
*Decided provisionally* with the ISMS (UC-ISMS-1, UC-ISMS-2, UC-SAMM-6, UC-TM-3; UC-ISMS-3 is the page of the lens *Reports*,
<<_decided_reports>>), so that no screen is blocked; the owner may change each one. The bodies and addresses are in
<<_isms_requests>>; the numbering goes on from the incident reports:

[start=51]
. *The addresses.* The lens *ISMS* gains `/isms/risks/new` (query `origin`), `/isms/documents/new` (query `kind`) and
  `/isms/documents/{ismsdocument}/compare` (queries `from`, `to`); `isms.documents` admits `kind`, `isms.document` admits `at`;
  `new` is reserved as an entry and as a document (the placeholder patterns `entry` and `ismsdocument`, the second case
  sensitive because a document id is a ULID, replacing `{id}`). The name `origin` is new in `queries` of
  `canonical-addresses.json`; `kind`, `at`, `from` and `to` are the existing names with their existing patterns. The compare
  page is an address of its own and not a query of the document sheet, so that a comparison can be linked and returns from a
  step-up like every address.
. *The start of the lens reads two lists and has no request of its own.* `/isms` shows the document types with their documents
  (`GET /api/v1/documents/isms-core`, grouped by `kind`) and the register (`GET /api/v1/isms/register`); a user who may read
  only one repository sees that area. The documents of a type are the generic list filtered by `kind` (`?kind=` asks the server
  when the list is `truncated`).
. *The five types are the generic lifecycle,* each in the repository of its type, released more than once, one workflow for the
  category: the transitions `isms.submit` (`draft` to `in-review`, right `edit`, no step-up) and `isms.release` (`in-review` to
  `released`, right `approve`, step-up), the tags `policies/<id>/v<n>`, `scope/<id>/v<n>`, `audits/<id>/v<n>`,
  `management-reviews/<id>/v<n>` and `risks/R-<n>/v<n>` (the collections of AUN-ISMS-1 and AUN-ISMS-2). The specification
  does not say whether a document is released once or more than once; "every earlier version stays comparable" (UC-ISMS-1)
  asks for more than once.
. *Every type is a single-file document with the part `data`;* the AsciiDoc fragments of AUN-ISMS-1 are fields of that file, not
  files of their own.
. *The generated output of AUN-ISMS-1 is not part of the web app.* AUN-ISMS-1 names it as part of the layout of a type and
  says nothing about who makes it or whether a person reads it; the web app shows the released data and the diff, and has no
  request for the output. The owner may want a generated page in the web app.
. *The fields of the types are provisional* (<<_isms_data>>), as is every list of values: a document has a title, an owner role,
  its main text (`statements`, `boundaries`, `conclusion`) and a review date; an audit report a year, a period, the auditors,
  findings with severity `major`, `minor` or `observation` and issue ids, and a conclusion; a management review a year, the date,
  participants, decisions with role, date and issue ids, and a conclusion; a risk a title, a description, a rating, a
  treatment, notes on the treatment, an owning team and an optional role, a review date, measures as issue ids, an origin and
  related references. Mandatory for the transition into review are the `required` of the type; the first save needs the title.
  The labels are the catalogue keys `category.isms.<kind>.field.<path>`.
. *Two versions are compared with the generic diff:* `GET …/diff?from=<revision>&to=<revision>`, where `from=released` stays and
  `to` defaults to the working state; no request of its own, and the page `/isms/documents/<id>/compare` reads it. The same
  request serves a risk, which has no compare page in this step (the owner may want one).
. *The register as it is worked on is `GET /api/v1/isms/register`.* The specified `GET /api/v1/isms/risks?as-of=` lists released
  entries only and is not changed; the lens needs the draft, the entries in review and the reopened ones, the rating and the
  review state, which the generic list does not carry. The list has the members of the as-of list plus the state, the release
  and the update, is ordered by number, is not paged and holds at most 500 entries (`truncated`).
. *A risk is the document `R-<n>` of `isms-register`,* read, saved, submitted and released with the generic requests; the
  address segment is the number in lower case and the web app upper-cases it for the requests (the register as of a date
  already names `entry` so). The release of an entry merges its path into `main` and tags it (AUN-ISMS-2, AUN-ISMS-3); the
  web app sees no branch.
. *The number is assigned by the first save, by the server.* `/isms/risks/new` stores nothing, shows no number, and moves to
  the address of the entry with `replace` after the `201`. A "next number" is never shown. The numbers are never reused and need
  not be consecutive (`R-021`, then `R-042`, then the next free one). Two creates at once get two numbers (UC-ISMS-2), which the
  mock shows by answering the number that the register read earlier did not have.
. *A first save needs only the title;* rating, treatment and owner are mandatory for the submit (`MANDATORY_FIELDS_MISSING`), as
  the main text of a document is. The incident reports take the same line (point 24 of the incidents: only a title).
. *The review date is typed, not computed.* No interval is derived from the rating (a threat model's interval comes from the
  protection need, AUN-TM-3); `reviewStatus` is `overdue`, `due-soon` (30 days) or `ok` by the date, as the Reports summary counts
  it. A risk or a document without a date has none.
. *The gaps of a scope are one request,* `GET /api/v1/samm/{scope}/gaps`, those of the latest released assessment (AUN-SAMM-6),
  every practice whose released score is below its target, in the order of the model, each with the reference, the score,
  the target and, when a risk of the register names the gap, that risk. The scope sheet shows them as a part of the
  sheet (the web app decides the place) and offers *Create risk* for a gap without a risk to a person with `edit` on
  `isms-register`; the practice sheet of a released assessment may offer it too. A scope without a released assessment or without
  targets has no gap.
. *The draft of a risk from an origin is one request,* `GET /api/v1/isms/risks/draft?origin=` (UC-SAMM-6 and UC-TM-3 "the
  server drafts a risk with the reference"): it stores nothing, and answers the origin and the prefilled fields. The prefill is
  the title and the description (for a gap the practice and the scores, for a threat the name and the description of the threat,
  in English), the owning team (the subject of the scope when it is a team; else the owner of the service), and the treatment
  `mitigate` for a gap and `accept` for an accepted threat. The address `/isms/risks/new?origin=` is the page of the form.
. *An origin is one risk at a time.* A gap or a threat that already has a risk is refused with `ORIGIN_HAS_RISK` (and the gaps
  list says so); a gap needs a released assessment and a target above its score (`ORIGIN_NOT_RELEASED`, `ORIGIN_NOT_A_GAP`); a
  threat is transferable when it is `accepted`, or `open` without a countermeasure and not transferred (AUN-TM-4:
  "accepted or untreated"; `ORIGIN_NOT_TRANSFERABLE`). Whether a second risk may name the same gap (a new
  assessment, a new gap on the same practice) is an owner question: the gap of the next released assessment is a different
  reference (the assessment is part of it), so it is a new gap.
. *The rating and the treatment are the person's.* The application derives no rating from the score of a gap or from the
  likelihood and impact of a threat, and no treatment but the two prefills of point 64: the scales are not comparable and the
  rating of a risk is the owner's judgement.
