. *The origin is a member of the create, not a field.* `POST /api/v1/documents/isms-register` accepts the optional member
  `origin` beside `kind` and `parts` (as the create of a threat model takes `recipe`); the server validates it as the draft does
  and writes `/origin` into the file, which the schema marks `readOnly`: the form does not offer it and a later save answers
  `READ_ONLY` for it. The answer carries `origin` `{ref, recorded}`.
. *The transfer is recorded in the threat model by the application, in a second commit.* AUN-TM-4 names the reference of the
  risk to the threat; the threat models (<<_decided_threat_models>>, point 18) keep the reference `transferred` on the threat,
  which the schema marks `readOnly`. The first save of the risk, when the number exists, writes it with a save of the part `model`
  as the person; a released model becomes `draft` with the next version, because it is a save of the file like any other. Two
  repositories are no transaction: a write that fails leaves the risk and answers `recorded: false`, the next save of the risk
  tries again. The person needs `edit` on `isms-register` and, for the write, nothing more: the application writes on their
  behalf. The alternative is to derive `transferred` from the register's `origin` and never store it in the model; the owner
  decides (for the owner, below).
. *The two acts are disabled with their reason without the right.* *Create risk* (the gap) and *Transfer to risk register* (the
  threat) need `edit` on `isms-register`; without it the control stays and says why (the design rule of a disabled control), and
  a person who may not read `isms-register` sees no risk of a gap. *Release* needs `approve` and step-up as every release does.
  The mock gives the user of the profile `workshop` the rights `read`, `edit` and `approve` on `isms-register` and `isms-core`, so
  that the release of a document and of a risk can be walked through (as for the incidents, point 11); `read-only` has `read` on
  both; `release` keeps `read` on `isms-register` and has none on `isms-core`, so that a half-right lens can be seen; `degraded`
  and `local` have `read` and `edit`.
. *There is no way to delete, retire or close a risk.* A risk that no longer applies is released with the treatment that says
  so (`accept`, `avoid`) and a review date, or not reviewed again. A retirement (a state `closed`, a request that withdraws the
  entry from the register as of a date) is an owner question.
. *The Due lens lists the reviews of released entries,* `kind` `review`, lens `isms`, for a review date that is overdue or within 30
  days (<<_isms_due>>); an entry in review is no item in this step. The titles are `Risk R-<n>: <title>` and the document's title.
. *The generic events, search and references cover the new objects:* `document-created` with `kind` `risk` or a document type; a
  hit of `GET /api/v1/search` for a document and for an entry (`id` `R-<n>`); `isms-register:risk/R-<n>` and
  `isms-core:<kind>/<ulid>` resolve to the title and the address. The transfer's write into the threat model is a `document-saved`
  of `threat-models` with `part` `model`.
. *Texts the server writes are English.* The prefilled title and description of a draft risk are written by the server in
  English, as the report of an incident is; the web app shows them as the person's own text to edit. The labels and the notices
  around them are the app's catalogue.
. *Reading while the Git server is unreachable* serves the register, the documents, the gaps and the reads from the last
  fetched state; the writes (the create and the save of a risk or a document, the transfer) answer `503 GIT_UNAVAILABLE` and keep the
  draft. The page of `/isms/risks/new` keeps the person's input.
. *The origin of a risk is hidden from a user who may not read its repository,* in the generic read as in the register: the
  server leaves `/origin` out of the value it returns (the working state, the draft, a release read with `at`, the `diff`),
  because the raw reference would name a restricted model or assessment (AUN-DP-2). The form does not offer the field, so no
  save changes.
. *A pending transfer is retried by the submit and the release, and blocks the release.* The first save may answer
  `origin.recorded: false` (the threat model could not be written); the submit and the release then try the write again as the
  person who acts, the submit goes on, the release answers `409 TRANSFER_PENDING` until the link is written. Otherwise a risk
  could be released without a save in between and leave its threat counted as untreated. Provisional until the owner decides
  between storing the link and deriving it (68), which would make the point void.
. *A save reopens a released risk; a release that somebody else made first is `DOCUMENT_RELEASED`.* A risk is released more than once, so
  a save is never refused for a released risk: it starts the next version (a draft, version 3 after the release v2) and the release
  stays readable with `?at=v2`. A release request for a risk that somebody released while the review was open answers `409
  DOCUMENT_RELEASED` with the `tag`, the outcome of the generic release request (decided provisionally with the release flow, like
  the other repositories; the mock state is `risk-release-released-meanwhile`, a save in it is `risk-release-reopened`). A release
  after a re-read diff that carries a change saved meanwhile (`risk-release-changed-reviewed`) releases that change with the version
  (`risk-released-changed`).

*Decided provisionally with the documents of the lens ISMS* (the web app, UC-ISMS-1; points 77 to 82 go on from 76; the risk
register is the next part of the lens):

[start=77]
. *The documents are three levels of the chain.* The first column lists the four document types of `isms-core` (*Policy*,
  *Scope of the ISMS*, *Audit report*, *Management review*, in that order) after a row *All documents*, each with how many
  documents it holds and how many are in review, and, below a hairline, the way into the risk register; the register's own
  figures are those of the next part. The second column is the list of one type (`/isms/documents?kind=`) or of every type
  (`/isms/documents`, newest change first, each row naming its type); the object is the sheet of a document, the form of a new
  one, or the sheet of an earlier state. The lens reads the one list `GET /api/v1/documents/isms-core` (point 52) and groups and
  counts it in the browser, so it has no request of its own. A row says the state, the release the document holds (`Last release
  v3, 20 May 2026`: a document is released more than once, so the version of the last release is how many there were) and, for a
  document that is not released, the working version with who changed it last. A person who may not read `isms-core` is told so in
  place of the lists and still finds the register.
. *The sheet and the release of a document are the generic ones, drawn on the addresses of the lens.* The category `isms` is no
  generic category (`GET /api/v1/categories` does not list it and the rail's lens *Documents* does not show it), so the web app
  knows the four types and the repository `isms-core` itself and reads the schema with `GET /api/v1/categories/isms/schema`
  (<<_isms_data>>); the sheet, the steps of the workflow, the conflicts, the earlier states (`?at=`) and the release page
  (`ReleaseFlow`: the diff since the last release `v<n>`, the step-up, the seal and the notary after the release) are the
  components of the generic lens with the addresses of this one. The labels are the catalogue keys `category.isms.<kind>.field.<path>`
  of the web app (point 56); the step *Submit for review* is `category.isms.transition.isms.submit`, the release reads *Release*.
. *The comparison is a page, and it compares releases.* `/isms/documents/<id>/compare?from=&to=` reads
  `GET …/diff?from=<revision>&to=<revision>` (<<_requests>>, point 57) with the references of the pickers,
  which are the releases of the document as `GET …/revisions` lists them (`v1`, `v2`, …); the later side may be the current
  state (the page then sends no `to`). Without `from` the page lists the pairs of neighbouring releases, newest first, besides the two
  pickers; a document with fewer than two releases says that a comparison needs two. The same version on both sides is
  refused on the page, without a request. The pickers do not offer the commits between two releases (they are read with `?at=` on
  the sheet), and the sheet offers the comparison of its two latest releases from the second release on. The changes are those of the
  release page, grouped by property and read as the form reads them (a person by the name, a reference by its title).
. *The review date is a fact under the title.* The sheet reads `/review-due` of the document's value and says where it stands
  against today (point 62): the date alone when it is more than 30 days ahead, the date and the days when it is within 30 days or
  overdue, in words, in the ink of the text, with no box and no colour of its own (a review that needs a person is the Due lens's
  item). An earlier state shows the date it held, with no standing. The list rows show no review date, because the summary of a
  document (`GET /api/v1/documents/{repository}`) has none.
. *A first save of a document needs only the title,* as the first save of a risk does (point 61): the other mandatory fields stop the
  submit (`MANDATORY_FIELDS_MISSING`), which the sheet lists as ways to the fields. A new document is the form of its type at
  `/isms/documents/new?kind=`, holds its value in the page until the first save, asks before the page is left, and moves to the
  address the `201` names in place of its own; without `kind` the four types are offered as a choice, and a type the category does
  not have (the risk among them) is the not-found view.
. *A policy that applies to the whole organisation names the reference `organisation`.* The data of the mock does so (`applies-to`
  of the three policies); no specification says what such a reference is. The mock's `GET /api/v1/references/resolve` answers it
  (title *Organisation*, no address) so that the sheet does not show *Not found* for a released policy.

For the owner, besides the questions of the earlier points: whether the summary of a document in `GET /api/v1/documents/{repository}`
should carry its review date, so that a list could show the reviews that fall due (80); whether the comparison should offer the commits
between two releases and the working state as the earlier side (79); whether `organisation` is a reference at all, or a policy that
applies to everyone should say so with a field of its own (82).

For the owner: whether the categories of the ISMS should be one workflow or one per type, and whether a document may be
released once only (53); whether a generated page of the ISMS documents belongs in the web app (55); the fields of the five
types, above all the mandatory ones, the vocabularies of the rating and the treatment (which are those of the Reports step, not
checked against a standard) and whether a risk should keep likelihood and impact and derive the rating from them (56, 66); whether
the risk register as it is worked on should be a request of the library's index and whether it needs a paged request at a few
thousand entries (58, with the open point of UC-ISMS-2: the measurement of the index at register size is still to be made); whether a
risk may have more than one origin or a gap more than one risk (65); whether the transfer should be stored in the threat model at
all, or derived from the register (68, 76; it makes a released model a draft and fails without a transaction); whether a risk
should be closed, retired or deleted, and whether an owner must accept a residual risk before the release (70; the requirement of
the standard is not checked here, only that the register names the owner by team and role); whether the Due lens should list the
entries in review and the reviews of drafts (71); whether the first save of a risk may be refused for the title alone being
absent (61); whether the application should keep a "next number" visible, which the first-save numbering of the library
forbids (60); and whether the release of a risk that somebody released meanwhile answers `DOCUMENT_RELEASED` as in the other repositories (the last
item).
